Lemma 14.39
.

The are natural equivalences of
\mathbb{Z}
-homology theories

\displaystyle H_{n}^{\mathbb{Z}}(-,{\mathbf{K}}^{\infty,\operatorname{W}}_{\matheurm{Ch}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*}))})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\cong}

\displaystyle H_{n}^{\mathbb{Z}}(-,\mathbf{K}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*})});

\displaystyle H_{n}^{\mathbb{Z}}(-,{\mathbf{K}}^{\infty,\operatorname{W}}_{\matheurm{Ch}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*}[t,t^{-1}]))})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\cong}

\displaystyle H_{n}^{\mathbb{Z}}(-,\mathbf{K}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*}[t,t^{-1}])}).

Proof.

They are induced by the zigzag of natural weak homotopy equivalences appearing
in Theorem
14.17

using
[
5
, Lemma 4.6]

∎

Lemma 14.40
.

For every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
there exists a commutative diagram

\textstyle{H_{n-1}^{\mathbb{Z}}(E\mathbb{Z},\mathbf{K}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*})})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{n-1}^{\mathbb{Z}}(\pt,\mathbf{K}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*})})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{n}^{\mathbb{Z}}(E\mathbb{Z},\mathbf{K}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*}[t,t^{-1}])})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{n}^{\mathbb{Z}}(\pt,\mathbf{K}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*}[t,t^{-1}])})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{n-1}^{\mathbb{Z}}(E\mathbb{Z},\mathbf{K}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*})})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{n-1}^{\mathbb{Z}}(\pt,\mathbf{K}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*})})}

such that the composite of the two vertical arrows appearing in the left
and in the right column are isomorphisms and the horizontal arrows are induced by the projection
E\mathbb{Z}\to\pt
.

In particular the upper horizontal arrow is bijective, if the middle horizontal arrow is bijective.

Proof.

Because of Lemma
14.39
it suffices to proof
Lemma
14.40
in the case, where we replace

\mathbf{K}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*})}
by
{\mathbf{K}}^{\infty,\operatorname{W}}_{\matheurm{Ch}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*}))}
and

\mathbf{K}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*}[t,t^{-1}])}
by
\mathbf{K}^{\infty}_{\matheurm{Ch}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*}[t,t^{-1}]))}
.

Now one easily checks unravelling the definitions that
the rows of the diagram above after this replacement
agree with the rows of the diagram (
14.37
).
Now the claim follows from the diagram (
14.37
).
∎

14.
m
.
Reduction to the connective case

Theorem 14.41

(Reduction to the connective case)
.

Fix a natural number
n_{0}
. Suppose that for every natural number
d
and
every natural number
n
satisfying
n\geq n_{0}
the map

H_{n}^{\mathbb{Z}}(E\mathbb{Z},\mathbf{K}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*}[\mathbb{Z}^{d}])})\to H_{n}^{\mathbb{Z}}(\pt,\mathbf{K}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*}[\mathbb{Z}^{d}])})=K_{n}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*}[\mathbb{Z}^{d}])\times_{\mathcal{L}(\Phi[\mathbb{Z}^{d}])}\mathbb{Z})

is an isomorphism.

Then for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
the map

H_{n}^{\mathbb{Z}}(E\mathbb{Z},\mathbf{K}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*})})\to H_{n}^{\mathbb{Z}}(\pt,\mathbf{K}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*})})=K_{n}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*})\times_{\mathcal{L}(\Phi)}\mathbb{Z})

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

One can iterate the passage from
\mathcal{A}_{*}
to
\mathcal{A}_{*}[t,t^{-1}]=\mathcal{A}_{*}[\mathbb{Z}]
and obtain a passage from

\mathcal{A}_{*}
to
\mathcal{A}_{*}[\mathbb{Z}^{d}]=(\mathcal{A}_{*}[\mathbb{Z}^{d-1}])[t,t^{-1}]
for every natural number
d
.
Now Theorem
14.41
follows from
Lemma
14.40
.
∎

14.
n
.
Proof of Theorem
14.1

Now we are ready to finalize the proof of Theorem
14.1
.

Thanks to Lemma
14.2
, we can assume without loss of generality that
r=1
.
Because of Theorem
14.41
it suffices to show
that for every natural number
d
and every natural number
n
satisfying
n\geq 2
the map

H_{n}^{\mathbb{Z}}(E\mathbb{Z},\mathbf{K}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*}[\mathbb{Z}^{d}])})\to H_{n}^{\mathbb{Z}}(\pt,\mathbf{K}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*}[\mathbb{Z}^{d}])})=K_{n}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*}[\mathbb{Z}^{d}])\times_{\mathcal{L}(\Phi[\mathbb{Z}^{d}])}\mathbb{Z})

is an isomorphism.

Let
\mathbf{K}_{\Idem(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*}[\mathbb{Z}^{d}]))}
be the connective
version of
\mathbf{K}^{\infty}_{\Idem(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*}[\mathbb{Z}^{d}]))}
. Since we have
\dim(E\mathbb{Z})\leq 1
, we conclude from
Lemma
3.3
that the vertical arrows appearing in the commutative diagram

\textstyle{H_{n}^{\mathbb{Z}}(E\mathbb{Z};\mathbf{K}_{\Idem(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*}[\mathbb{Z}^{d}]))})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{H_{n}^{\mathbb{Z}}(\pt;\mathbf{K}_{\Idem(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*}[\mathbb{Z}^{d}]))})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{H_{n}^{\mathbb{Z}}(E\mathbb{Z};\mathbf{K}^{\infty}_{\Idem(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*}[\mathbb{Z}^{d}]))})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{n}^{\mathbb{Z}}(\pt;\mathbf{K}^{\infty}_{\Idem(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*}[\mathbb{Z}^{d}]))})}
\textstyle{H_{n}^{\mathbb{Z}}(E\mathbb{Z};\mathbf{K}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*}[\mathbb{Z}^{d}])})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{H_{n}^{\mathbb{Z}}(\pt;\mathbf{K}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*}[\mathbb{Z}^{d}])})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}

are bijective for
n\geq 2
. Hence it suffices show that for every natural number
d
the map

(14.42)

H_{n}^{\mathbb{Z}}(E\mathbb{Z};\mathbf{K}_{\Idem(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*}[\mathbb{Z}^{d}]))})\to H_{n}^{\mathbb{Z}}(\pt;\mathbf{K}_{\Idem(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*}[\mathbb{Z}^{d}]))})

is bijective for
n\in\mathbb{Z},n\geq 2
.

By assumption the category
\mathcal{A}_{m}[\mathbb{Z}^{d}]
is uniformly
l(d)
-regular coherent and the
inclusion
\mathcal{A}_{m}[\mathbb{Z}^{d}]\to\mathcal{A}_{m+1}[\mathbb{Z}^{d}]
is flat for every
m\geq 0
. We conclude
from Lemma
13.10
that
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*}[\mathbb{Z}^{d}])
is
uniformly
l(d)
-regular coherent. We conclude from
Lemma
6.4

(vi)

that
\Idem(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*}[\mathbb{Z}^{d}]))
is uniformly
l(d)
-regular coherent. Hence

\Idem(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*}[\mathbb{Z}^{d}]))
is idempotent complete and regular coherent.
Now the bijectivity of (
14.42
) for

n\in\mathbb{Z},n\geq 2
follows from
Theorem
7.5
applied to
\Idem(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{*}[\mathbb{Z}^{d}]))
,
since for the map
\mathbf{a}
appearing there the homomorphism
\pi_{n}(\mathbf{a})
can be identified with the
map (
14.42
) for
n\geq 2
. This finishes the proof of
Theorem
14.1
.
